Fight Night: Gustafsson vs. Teixeira|date= {{start date|2017|05|28}}\n|venue=[[Ericsson Globe]]\n|city=[[Stockholm]], [[Sweden]]\n|image=UFC_Stockholm_2017.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting 212|UFC 212: Aldo vs. Holloway]]\n}}\n'''''UFC Fight Night: Gustafsson vs. Teixeira''''' (also known as '''''UFC Fight Night 109''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event produced by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] held on May 28, 2017, at [[Ericsson Globe]] in [[Stockholm]], [[Sweden]].{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2017/02/alexander-gustafsson-vs-glover-teixeira-headlines-ufc-fight-night-in-stockholm|title=Alexander Gustafsson vs. Glover Teixeira headlines UFC Fight Night in Stockholm|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2017-02-28|access-date=2017-02-28}}\n\n==Background==\nPreliminary reports indicated that the event was expected to be contested at the newly opened [[Royal Arena]] in [[Copenhagen]], which would have marked the promotion's first visit to [[Denmark]].{{cite news|url=http://www.mmamad.com/2017/01/28/ufc-to-make-promotional-debut-in-copenhagen-denmark-on-may-27-2017/|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170202073900/http://www.mmamad.com/2017/01/28/ufc-to-make-promotional-debut-in-copenhagen-denmark-on-may-27-2017/|url-status=usurped|archive-date=February 2, 2017|title=UFC to make promotional debut in Copenhagen, Denmark on May 27, 2017|publisher=mmamad.com|author=Dale Jordan|date=2017-01-28|access-date=2017-01-28}} However, on February 28, the promotion announced that the event would take place in Stockholm.{{cite news|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/Gustafsson-vs-Teixeira-set-for-UFC-return-to-Stockholm|title=Gustafsson vs. Teixeira set for UFC return to Stockholm|publisher=ufc.com|author=Staff|date=2017-02-28|access-date=2017-02-28}}\n\nA light heavyweight bout between former [[UFC Light Heavyweight Championship]] challengers [[Alexander Gustafsson]] and [[Glover Teixeira]] served as the main event. The pairing was previously scheduled to headline [[UFC Fight Night 69]] in June 2015. However, Gustafsson pulled out of that fight due to injury.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2015/05/alexander-gustafsson-out-vs-teixeira-jedrzejczyk-penne-headlines-ufc-fight-night-69|title=Gustafsson out vs. Teixeira, Jedrzejczyk vs. Penne headlines UFC Fight Night 69|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|access-date=2015-05-01|date=2015-05-01}}\n\n[[Magnus Cedenblad]] was expected to face [[Chris Camozzi]] at the event. However, Cedenblad was removed from the card on March 27 and was replaced by [[Trevor Smith (fighter)|Trevor Smith]].{{cite news|url=http://mmadna.nl/trevor-smith-vervangt-magnus-cedenblad-in-stockholm-tegen-chris-camozzi|title=Trevor Smith replaces Magnus Cedenblad against Chris Camozzi at UFC Stockholm|publisher=mmadna.nl|author=Marcel Dorff|date=2017-03-27|access-date=2017-03-27|language=nl}}\n\n[[Emil Weber Meek]] was expected to face [[Nordine Taleb]] at the event. However, Meek pulled out of the fight on May 12 citing injury.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2017/05/emil-meek-ufc-fight-night-109-fight-nordine-taleb-sweden|title=Emil Meek says he's out of UFC Fight Night 109 fight with Nordine Taleb in Sweden|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|access-date=2017-05-12|date=2017-05-12}} He was replaced by promotional newcomer [[Oliver Enkamp]].{{cite news|url=https://www.mmanytt.se/senaste-nytt/uppgifter-oliver-enkamp-klar-for-ufc-stockholm-ersatter-emil-valhalla-meek/|title=Oliver Enkamp ready for UFC Stockholm – replaces Emil \"Valhalla\" Meek|publisher=mmanytt.se|author=Ashah Tafari|date=2017-05-12|access-date=2017-05-16|language=sv}}\n\nA heavyweight bout between Christian Colombo and [[Damian Grabowski]] was expected to take place at the event. However, the pairing was scrapped as both fighters sustained injuries in the weeks leading up to the event.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2017/05/injuries-christian-colombo-damian-grabowski-ufc-fight-night-109-sweden|title=Injuries scrap Christian Colombo vs. Damian Grabowski at UFC Fight Night 109 in Sweden|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|access-date=2017-05-16|date=2017-05-16}}\n\n[[Mairbek Taisumov]] was expected to face [[Joaquim Silva (fighter)|Joaquim Silva]] at the event. However, Taisumov pulled out of the fight on May 16 citing a knee injury.{{cite web|url=http://mmadna.nl/nieuwe-tegenvaller-ufc-stockholm-mairbek-taisumov-out/|title=UFC Stockholm: Mairbek Taisumov out|publisher=mmadna.nl|author=Marcel Dorff|access-date=2017-05-16|date=2017-05-16|language=nl}} He was replaced by [[Reza Madadi]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2017/05/reza-madadi-mairbek-taisumov-joaquim-silva-ufc-fight-night-109-stockholm-sweden|title=Reza Madadi steps in for Mairbek Taisumov, meets Joaquim Silva at UFC Fight Night 109 in Sweden|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|access-date=2017-05-19|date=2017-05-19}}\n\nAt the weigh-ins, [[Darren Till]] came in at 176 lb, five pounds over the welterweight limit of 171 lb. As a result, he was fined 20% of his purse, which went to his opponent [[Jessin Ayari]] and the bout proceeded at a catchweight.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2017/05/ufc-fight-night-109-official-weigh-in-results-and-live-video-stream-9-a-m-et|title=UFC Fight Night 109 official weigh-in results: Gustafsson, Teixeira cleared for headliner|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2017-05-27|access-date=2017-05-27}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (Fox Sports 1)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Light of Champions","revisions":[{"slots":{"main":{"contentmodel":"wikitext","contentformat":"text/x-wiki","content":"{{short description|UFC mixed martial arts television series and event in 2016}}\n{{Use mdy dates|date=May 2018}}\n{{Infobox television\n|image = \n|genre = Reality, [[Broadcasting of sports events|Sports]]\n|runtime = 60 minutes\n|creator = [[Frank Fertitta III]], [[Lorenzo Fertitta]], [[Dana White]]\n|starring = [[Dana White]], [[Joseph Benavidez]], [[Henry Cejudo]]\n|country = United States\n|network = [[Fox Sports 1]]\n|first_aired = {{Start date|2016|08|31}}\n|last_aired = \n}}\n\t\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter: Tournament of Champions''''' (also known as '''''The Ultimate Fighter 24''''' and '''Team Benavidez vs. Team Cejudo''') is an installment of the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC)-produced reality television series ''[[The Ultimate Fighter]]''.{{cite news|url=http://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Joseph-Benavidez-Henry-Cejudo-to-Serve-as-Coaches-on-The-Ultimate-Fighter-24-104813|title=Joseph Benavidez, Henry Cejudo to serve as coaches on 'The Ultimate Fighter 24'|publisher=sherdog.com|author=Tristen Critchfield|date=May 11, 2016|access-date=2016-05-11}}{{cite news|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/Flyweights-to-be-featured-on-Season-24-of-TUF|title=Flyweights to be featured on The Ultimate Fighter Season 24|publisher=ufc.com|author=Thomas Gerbasi|date=May 11, 2016|access-date=2016-05-11}}\n\nOn May 11, 2016 the UFC announced that the 16 competitors for the season would be made up of [[Flyweight (MMA)|flyweight]] fighters from various locations who are champions in their respective organizations around the world, with the winner being expected to have a chance to fight for the [[UFC Flyweight Championship]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/05/tuf-24-gets-other-flyweight-champs-with-coaches-benavidez-cejudo-winner-fights-champ-johnson|title='TUF 24' gets other flyweight champs with coaches Benavidez, Cejudo; winner fights champ Johnson|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Matt Erickson|date=May 11, 2016|access-date=2016-05-11}} The cast was announced on July 20.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/07/tuf-24-cast-revealed-includes-legacy-rfa-and-shooto-champs-vying-for-title-shot|title='TUF 24' cast revealed, includes Legacy, RFA and Shooto champs vying for title shot|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Matt Erickson|date=July 20, 2016|access-date=2016-07-20}}\n\nThe coaches for the season were former flyweight title challengers [[Joseph Benavidez]] and [[Henry Cejudo]].\n\n==Cast==\n\n===Teams===\n{{Col-begin}}\n{{Col-break}}\n;{{color box|#E5E4E2|border=silver}} {{color box|#0070FF|border=silver}} '''Team Benavidez''':\n*[[Joseph Benavidez]], Head Coach\n*[[Robert Drysdale]]\n*[[Danny Castillo]]\n*Eddie Barraco\n*Robert Follis\n\n{{Col-break}} {{color box|black|border=silver}} {{color box|#FDEE00|border=silver}} '''Team Cejudo''':\n*[[Henry Cejudo]], Head Coach\n*[[Eric Albarracin]]\n*Víctor Dávila\n*Kirian Fitzgibbons\n*Gastón Bolaños\n\n{{col-end}}\n\n===Fighters===\n*'''Team Benavidez'''\n**[[Tim Elliott]], [[Brandon Moreno]], [[Damacio Page]], [[Eric Shelton (fighter)|Eric Shelton]], Ronaldo Candido, Matt Rizzo, [[Hiromasa Ougikubo]] and Terrence Mitchell.\n*'''Team Cejudo'''\n**Charlie Alaniz, [[Alexandre Pantoja]], Adam Antolin, Yoni Sherbatov, Jamie Alvarez, [[Matt Schnell (fighter)|Matt Schnell]], Nkazimulo Zulu and [[Kai Kara-France]].\n\n==Episodes==\n'''Episode 1: The Tournament Begins (August 31, 2016)'''{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/04/episode-no-1-recap-the-ultimate-fighter-23-team-joanna-vs-team-claudia |title=Episode No. 1 recap: 'The Ultimate Fighter 23: Team Joanna vs. Team Claudia'|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Mike Bohn|date=April 20, 2016|access-date=2016-04-20}}\n* The fighters arrive in Las Vegas and some of them are introduced via confessionals. All of the contestants are currently champions in their organizations.\n* UFC President [[Dana White]] and coaches [[Joseph Benavidez]] and [[Henry Cejudo]] arrive at the gym. They welcome the fighters, but before further procedures are taken, [[UFC Flyweight Championship|UFC Flyweight Champion]] [[Demetrious Johnson]] is called by White. He delivers a message to the group stating that he hopes they learn a lot and he looks forward to defend his belt on the finale.\n* Johnson leaves and White explains what's going to happen: The fighters will split up for evaluation into various drills with Benavidez, Cejudo and their individual coaching staffs judging skills before team selections.\n* White kicked off the tournament by flipping a coin (blue for Benavidez, yellow for Cejudo) to determine which team will pick the first fighter. Team Benavidez wins the coin toss and White reveals the fighters have been seeded by the UFC for the tournament (which was unknown to the coaches). As a coach makes a pick, the corresponding fighter based on seed will go to the other coach (e.g., if the #1 seeded fighter is picked by a coach, the #16 seeded fighter will go to the other coach).\n* The eight coaches' picks (and corresponding seed sent to the other team as well as the organizations they are champions):\n\n:'''Tim Elliott''' ([[Titan Fighting Championships]]) – Ranked #3 (Team Benavidez – first pick)\n:'''Charlie Alaniz''' (Hex Fight Series – Australia) – Ranked #14 (Team Cejudo)\n\n:'''Alexandre Pantoja''' ([[Resurrection Fighting Alliance]]) – Ranked #1 (Team Cejudo – second pick)\n:'''Brandon Moreno''' ([[World Fighting Federation]]) – Ranked #16 (Team Benavidez)\n\n:'''Damacio Page''' ([[Legacy Fighting Championship]]) – Ranked #4 (Team Benavidez – third pick)\n:'''Adam Antolin''' ([[Tachi Palace Fights]]) – Ranked #13 (Team Cejudo)\n\n:'''Yoni Sherbatov''' (Xcessive Force Fighting Championship – Canada) – Ranked #2 (Team Cejudo – fourth pick)\n:'''Eric Shelton''' (Caged Aggression MMA)– Ranked #15 (Team Benavidez)\n\n:'''Ronaldo Candido''' ([[Shooto]] – South America) – Ranked #7 (Team Benavidez – fifth pick)\n:'''Jamie Alvarez''' (Absolute Fighting Championship) – Ranked #10 (Team Cejudo)\n\n:'''Matt Schnell''' ([[Legacy Fighting Championship]] – interim) – Ranked #6 (Team Cejudo – sixth pick)\n:'''Matt Rizzo''' (Ring of Combat)– Ranked #11 (Team Benavidez)\n\n:'''Hiromasa Ogikubo''' ([[Shooto]] – Japan) – Ranked #5 (Team Benavidez – seventh pick)\n:'''Nkazimulo Zulu''' (Extreme Fighting Championship – South Africa) – Ranked #12 (Team Cejudo)\n\n:'''Kai Kara-France''' (Bragging Rights Fight Series – Australia) – Ranked #9 (Team Cejudo – eight pick)\n:'''Terrence Mitchell''' (Alaska Fighting Championship) – Ranked #8 (Team Benavidez)\n\n* The fighters leave the gym and head back to the \"TUF\" house for the first time. The language and cultural barrier between the athletes is evident from the outset, but everyone is willing to endure the situation as they focus on the end prize.\n* At the first Team Benavidez training session, coach Benavidez makes it clear to his fighters he's not looking to change anyone's fighting style or approach to the sport. He simply wants to maximize the training environment for the athletes so they are at their best. Many of the fighters embrace him and believe he will be the superior coach with the superior team. Meanwhile, Team Cejudo follows with its first training session, and unsurprisingly the team led by the [[Wrestling at the 2008 Summer Olympics – Men's freestyle 55 kg|2008 Olympic gold medalist]] starts with wrestling drills. Some fighters express disappointment they didn't end up on Team Benavidez, but by the end of practice are generally pleased with where they ended up.\n* Back at the house, upcoming opponents Pantoja and Moreno end up on the balcony together and are interacting. Although one is from Brazil and the other is from Mexico, they find a way to communicate due to similar languages and discover their backgrounds have many common traits.\n* As the fighters finalize their preparation, Cejudo is already struggling with a coaching compromise. Moreno is one of his teammates and close friends from Arizona, and therefore he does not feel comfortable coaching Pantoja against him in the fight. He wishes Pantoja the best of luck but informs him that he will be removing himself from corner duties.\n* '''Alexandre Pantoja''' defeated Brandon Moreno via submission (rear-naked choke) in the second round.\n* The focus immediately shifts to the next bout, where #8 Mitchell will face off against #9 Kara-France. Both fighters finalize their preparation, trying to shore up any holes in their game that could be exposed in the upcoming contest. Cejudo expresses some concern about Kara-France, who he believes has some confidence issues going into his matchup.\n* '''Kai Kara-France''' defeated Terrence Mitchell via KO (punch) in the first round.\n* The next fight is announced: #5 Hiromasa Ogikubo vs. #12 Nkazimulo Zulu.\n\n'''Episode 2: Language of Combat (September 7, 2016)'''\n* '''Hiromasa Ogikubo''' defeated Nkazimulo Zulu via submission (rear-naked choke) in the second round.\n* The next fight is announced: #4 Damacio Page vs. #13 Adam Antolin.\n\n'''Episode 3: Look Through Your Soul (September 14, 2016)'''\n* '''Adam Antolin''' defeated Damacio Page via TKO (body kick and punches) in the second round.\n* The next fight is announced: #2 Yoni Sherbatov vs. #15 Eric Shelton.\n\n'''Episode 4: Young and Hungry (September 21, 2016)'''\n*Wanting their fighters to learn from their mistakes, Coaches Benavidez and Cejudo both hold a film session by showing their teams footage of their losses to current UFC flyweight champion [[Demetrious Johnson (fighter)|Demetrious Johnson]]. \n* '''Eric Shelton''' defeated Yoni Sherbatov via technical submission (rear-naked choke) in the second round.\n* The next fight is announced: #7 Ronaldo Candido vs. #10 Jamie Alvarez.\n\n'''Episode 5: Nothing Stops Us (September 28, 2016)'''\n*As a reward for their hard work, Dana White lets the fighters watch [[UFC 200]] at the ''TUF'' house.\n*UFC Heavyweight contender [[Travis Browne]] stops by the house to show off Harley-Davidson's custom motorcycles. He also informs them, \"In honor of the 24th season of ''TUF'', the winner and somebody that inspires them is going to get a brand new 2017 Harley-Davidson and sign them up for the riding academy.\"\n*'''Ronaldo Candido''' defeated Jamie Alvarez via Submission (rear-naked choke) in the first round.\n* The next fight is announced: #6 Matt Schnell vs. #11 Matt Rizzo.\n\n'''Episode 6: Let It Loose (October 5, 2016)'''\n*Coach Benavidez wants his team to take a break from training and getting away from the ''TUF'' house and rewards them with a day [[paddle boarding]] on [[Lake Las Vegas]].\n*'''Matt Schnell''' defeated Matt Rizzo via submission (triangle choke) in the second round.\n* The next fight is announced: #3 Tim Elliott vs. #14 Charlie Alaniz.\n\n'''Episode 7: Animal Instincts (October 12, 2016)'''\n*After the fighters relax in the hot tub and start drinking at the ''TUF'' house, Jamie Alvarez and Matt Rizzo get into a heated argument over their fight performances. \n*Emotions are high during Team Cejudo's training session when Charlie Alaniz starts smack talking about how he's going to \"knock out\" Kai Kara-France, his training partner. An annoyed Kai then brings it up after practice to the coaches. \n*This season's coaches challenge takes them to ''Top Golf'' [[driving range]]. Each coach must hit 20 golf balls in ring-shaped targets, trying to get the ball as close they can to the center target in order to score the most points. The farther away the target, the higher the score. And the highest score is a hole in one at the center of the farthest target. The first coach to earn 100 points wins a trophy and $10,000 ($1,500 per fighter) for their team. After 19 rounds, Benavidez wins the challenge with a score of 144 to 28.\n*'''Tim Elliott''' defeated Charlie Alaniz by submission (bulldog choke) in the first round.\n*The fights for the next round are announced: \n: #1 Alexandre Pantoja vs. #9 Kai Kara-France.\n: #5 Hiromasa Ogikubo vs. #13 Adam Antolin.\n: #7 Ronaldo Candido vs. #15 Eric Shelton.\n: #3 Tim Elliott vs. #6 Matt Schnell.\n\n'''Episode 8: Round Two (November 2, 2016)'''\n*The coaches reward the fighters for their hard work by throwing them a pool party at the ''TUF'' house.\n*Joe Benavidez invites former UFC fighter [[Duane Ludwig]] to help train not only his team but Henry Cejudo's team as well. There's just one condition; Cejudo cannot be there during the training session, much to the dislike of Cejudo's assistant coach Kirian Fitzgobbins who is Ludwig's friend. \n*'''Alexandre Pantoja''' defeated Kai Kara-France via unanimous decision after two rounds.\n*The next fight is #5 Hiromasa Ogikubo vs. #13 Adam Antolin.\n\n'''Episode 9: What Are You Fighting For? (November 9, 2016)'''\n*The coaches visit the ''TUF'' house to throw the fighters a \"grill-out\". \n*Things get heated after a night of drinking, especially between Eric Shelton and Tim Elliott when they disagree on what they are fighting for.\n*'''Hiromasa Ogikubo''' defeated Adam Antolin via unanimous decision after two rounds.\n*The next fights are announced:\n: #7 Ronaldo Candido vs. #15 Eric Shelton. \n: #3 Tim Elliott vs. #6 Matt Schnell.\n\n'''Episode 10: All On The Line (November 16, 2016)'''\n*Coach Benavidez brings in former Bantamweight champion and teammate [[TJ Dillashaw]] to provide an unbiased coaching approach for the upcoming bout between teammates.\n*'''Eric Shelton''' defeated Ronaldo Candido via unanimous decision after two rounds.\n*'''Tim Elliott''' defeated Matt Schnell via submission (front naked choke) in the first round.\n*The fights for the next round are announced:\n: #1 Alexandre Pantoja vs. #5 Hiromasa Ogikubo.\n: #15 Eric Shelton vs. #3 Tim Elliott.\n\n'''Episode 11: About To Clash (November 23, 2016)'''\n*The first semifinal fight immediately takes place.\n*'''Hiromasa Ogikubo''' defeated Alexandre Pantoja via unanimous decision after two rounds.\n*With Ogikubo's win no members of Team Cejudo remain in the competition.\n*'''Tim Elliott''' defeated Eric Shelton via majority decision after two rounds.\n*Coach Benavidez and many others believed the fight should have gone to a third round.\n*The final fight of the competition is announced with the winner receiving a title shot at Demetrious Johnson:\n: #5 Hiromasa Ogikubo vs #3 Tim Elliott.\n\n'''Episode 12: Last Champion Standing (November 30, 2016)'''{{Cite web |last=Bohn |first=Mike |title=Episode No. 12 recap: 'The Ultimate Fighter 24: Tournament of Champions' |url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/story/sports/mma/2016/11/30/episode-no-12-recap-the-ultimate-fighter-24-tournament-of-champions/82304146007/ |access-date=2025-07-27 |website=MMA Junkie |language=en-US}}\n*Elliott and Ogikubo are allowed to make calls to their families as reward for making it to the final round of the tournament.\n*Team Benavidez has a final dinner together discussing their time together before the final fight is underway.\n*'''Tim Elliott''' defeated Hiromasa Ogikubo via unanimous decision after two rounds.\n*Tim Elliott wins the tournament and earns the title shot at Demetrious Johnson.\n\n==Tournament bracket==\n{{Round16\n\n\n||{{color box|black|border=silver}} '''1 Alexandre Pantoja'''|'''SUB'''|{{color box|#E5E4E2|border=silver}} 16 Brandon (Technical) Knock Out\n|}\n{{Clear}}\n\n==The Ultimate Fighter 24 Finale==\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name=The Ultimate Fighter: Tournament of Champions Finale\n|image=TUF_24_Finale.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date=December 3, 2016\n|venue=[[Palms Casino Resort]]\n|city= [[Las Vegas Valley|Las Vegas]], [[Nevada]]\n|attendance=2,044{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/12/tuf-24-finale-draws-announced-2044-fans-for-188602-live-gate-in-las-vegas|title=TUF 24 Finale draws announced 2,044 fans for $188,602 live gate in Las Vegas|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=December 4, 2016}}\n|gate=$188,602\n|previousevent=[[UFC Fight Night: Whittaker vs. Brunson]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC Fight Night: Lewis vs. Abdurakhimov]]\n}}\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter: Tournament of Champions Finale''''' (also known as '''''The Ultimate Fighter 24 Finale''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event promoted by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] held on December 3, 2016, at [[Palms Casino Resort]] in [[Las Vegas Valley|Las Vegas]], Nevada.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/08/ufc-targeting-palms-in-las-vegas-for-tuf-24-finale-and-flyweight-title-fight|title=UFC targeting Palms in Las Vegas for TUF 24 Finale and flyweight title fight|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=August 18, 2016|access-date=2016-08-18}}\n\n===Background===\nThe event was headlined by a [[UFC Flyweight Championship]] bout between UFC champion [[Demetrious Johnson]] and [[Tim Elliott]], the winner of the flyweight tournament from ''The Ultimate Fighter 24''. The ''TUF'' winner also received a [[Harley-Davidson]] motorcycle of his choosing and a six-figure UFC contract.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/05/demetrious-johnson-to-defend-flyweight-title-against-wilson-reis-at-ufc-201|title=Demetrious Johnson to defend flyweight title against Wilson Reis at UFC 201|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=May 18, 2016|access-date=2016-05-18}}\n\nAlso, a flyweight bout between season coaches and former title challengers [[Joseph Benavidez]] and [[Henry Cejudo]] served as the co-main event.\n\n[[Jake Collier]] was expected to face Josh Stansbury at the event. However, Collier pulled out of the fight in late October citing injury and was replaced by [[Devin Clark (fighter)|Devin Clark]].{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/10/the-ultimate-fighter-24-finale-lineup-undergoes-a-few-changes|title=The Ultimate Fighter 24 Finale lineup undergoes a few changes|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=October 28, 2016|access-date=2016-10-28}}\n\n[[The Ultimate Fighter: Latin America]] bantamweight winner [[Alejandro Pérez (fighter)|Alejandro Pérez]] was scheduled to face [[Rob Font]] at the event, but pulled out on November 24.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/11/alejandro-perez-out-at-the-ultimate-fighter-24-finale-rob-font-needs-new-opponent|title=Alejandro Perez out at The Ultimate Fighter 24 Finale, Rob Font needs new opponent|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=John Morgan|date=November 24, 2016|access-date=2016-11-24}} He was replaced by promotional newcomer and this Ultimate Fighter season's contestant [[Matt Schnell]].{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/11/matt-schnell-gets-late-replacement-spot-on-saturdays-tuf-24-finale-fight-card|title=Matt Schnell gets late-replacement spot on Saturday's TUF 24 Finale fight card|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=November 27, 2016|access-date=2016-11-27}}\n\n===Results===\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (Fox Sports 1)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Flyweight\n|[[Demetrious Johnson]] (c)\n|def.\n|[[Tim Elliott]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (49–46, 49–46, 49–45)\n|5\n|5:00\n|For the [[UFC Flyweight Championship]].\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Flyweight\n|[[Joseph Benavidez]]\n|def.\n|[[Henry Cejudo]]\n|Decision (split) (27–29, 30–26, 29–27)\n|3\n|5:00\n|Cejudo was deducted one point in round 1 due to repeated low blows.\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Welterweight\n|[[Jorge Masvidal]]\n|def.\n|[[Jake Ellenberger]]\n|TKO (punches)\n|1\n|4:05\n|Ellenberger caught his foot in the fence, and was punched while trying to free it. Ferguson]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC Fight Night: Mousasi vs. Hall 2]]\n}}\n'''''UFC 205: Alvarez vs. McGregor''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] event promoted by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] held on November 12, 2016, at [[Madison Square Garden]] in [[New York City]], [[New York (state)|New York]].{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/04/mma-now-officially-legal-in-new-york-ufc-slates-nov-12-event-at-madison-square-garden|title=MMA now officially legal in New York; UFC slates Nov. 12 event at Madison Square Garden|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-04-14|access-date=2016-07-31}}\n\nIn what was described by UFC president [[Dana White]] as the \"biggest fight card\" in the company's history,{{cite news|url=https://nypost.com/2016/11/12/best-card-in-the-history-of-the-sport-ufc-205-invades-msg/|title='Best card in the history of the sport': UFC 205 invades MSG|publisher=nypost.com|author=George Willis|date=2016-11-12|access-date=2016-11-16}} [[Conor McGregor]] became the first fighter in the promotion's history to hold titles in two divisions simultaneously.{{cite news|url=https://www.telegraph.co.uk/mma/2016/11/12/eddie-alvarez-vs-conor-mcgregor-ufc-205-live/ |archive-url=https://ghostarchive.org/archive/20220112/https://www.telegraph.co.uk/mma/2016/11/12/eddie-alvarez-vs-conor-mcgregor-ufc-205-live/ |archive-date=2022-01-12 |url-access=subscription |url-status=live|title=UFC 205: Conor McGregor knocks out Eddie Alvarez in round two to claim historic second title|publisher=telegraph.co.uk|author=Gareth A. Davies and Chris Graham|date=2016-11-13|access-date=2016-11-16}}{{cbignore}}{{cite news|url=https://www.mirror.co.uk/sport/other-sports/mma/ufc-205-live-mcgregor-alvarez-9249314|title=UFC 205 LIVE Conor McGregor beats Eddie Alvarez with stunning second round TKO to win title|publisher=mirror.co.uk|author=Liam Williams|date=2016-11-13|access-date=2016-11-16}}\n\n==Background==\nThis event was the first UFC event hosted in [[New York City]]. It was also the first UFC event hosted in the state of [[New York (state)|New York]] after the longtime professional MMA ban was revoked in early 2016. To this point, [[UFC 7]] was the only event contested in the state, taking place in September 1995 in [[Buffalo, New York|Buffalo]].\n\nThe UFC worked tirelessly to surpass the law that forbade professional mixed martial arts events in the state, including a drastic measure: they announced an event ([[UFC 197]]) for the [[Madison Square Garden]], despite the ban still being in effect.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmamania.com/2015/9/28/9410591/ufc-sues-state-new-york-books-ppv-event-april-23-msg-mma|title=UFC sues state of New York, books PPV event for April 23 in Madison Square Garden|publisher=mmamania.com|author=Jesse Holland|date=2015-09-28|access-date=2016-07-31}} However, it was announced that a preliminary injunction allowing the promotion to hold the event was denied by a federal judge.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/01/ny-federal-judge-denies-ufc-move-to-allow-madison-square-garden-event|title=NY federal judge denies UFC move to allow Madison Square Garden event|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Steven Marrocco|date=2016-01-25|access-date=2016-07-31}} The event was eventually moved and contested in the promotion's home base of [[Las Vegas, Nevada]].\n\n===Main card: three title bouts, including a champion vs. champion contest===\n{{multiple image\n | align = left\n | total_width = 320\n | image1 = Eddie Alvarez.jpg\n | width1 = 1642 | height1 = 1917\n | alt1 =\n | caption1 =\n | image2 = Conor McGregor, UFC 189 World Tour London.jpg\n | width2 = 1870 | height2 = 2800\n | alt2 =\n | caption2 =\n | footer = Alvarez ''(left)'' had his first title defense, while McGregor ''(right)'' has not defended his since winning it against [[José Aldo]] at [[UFC 194]] in December 2015.\n}}\n\nThe event was headlined by a [[UFC Lightweight Championship]] bout between then champion [[Eddie Alvarez]] and [[UFC Featherweight Championship|UFC Featherweight Champion]] [[Conor McGregor]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/conor-mcgregor-gets-lightweight-title-shot-challenges-eddie-alvarez-in-ufc-205-headliner|title=Conor McGregor gets lightweight title shot, challenges Eddie Alvarez in UFC 205 headliner|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Mike Bohn|date=2016-09-27|access-date=2016-09-27}} This was the second time in UFC history that champions in different divisions fought for the same title. The first time was at [[UFC 94]] on January 31, 2009, when then-welterweight champion [[Georges St-Pierre]] defended his title against then-lightweight champion [[B.J. Penn]].\n\nAlvarez was originally expected to make his first title defense against undefeated contender [[Khabib Nurmagomedov]], but it was announced on September 21 that Alvarez missed the contract deadline for the bout and in consequence it was expected to take place at [[UFC 206]] in December.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/report-after-missed-contract-deadline-for-ufc-205-alvarez-vs-nurmagomedov-targeted-for-ufc-206|title=Report: After missed contract deadline for UFC 205, Alvarez vs. Nurmagomedov targeted for UFC 206|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-09-21|access-date=2016-09-21}} Meanwhile, McGregor was previously expected to challenge for the lightweight title at [[UFC 196]] in February against then champion [[Rafael dos Anjos]]. Dos Anjos eventually pulled out due to a broken foot and the bout never materialized.{{cite news|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2016/2/23/11098398/rafael-dos-anjos-injured-out-of-ufc-196-main-event-fight-against|title=Rafael dos Anjos injured, out of UFC 196 main event fight against Conor McGregor|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Ariel Helwani|date=2016-02-23|access-date=2016-09-27}}\n\nA [[UFC Welterweight Championship]] bout between the champion [[Tyron Woodley]] and five-time [[kickboxing]] world champion [[Stephen Thompson (fighter)|Stephen Thompson]] took place at the co-main event.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmanews.com/tyron-woodley-confirms-title-defense-against-wonderboy-thompson-at-ufc-205-in-ny/|title=Tyron Woodley confirms title defense against \"wonderboy\" Thompson at UFC 205 in NY|publisher=mmanews.com|author=Matt Boone|date=2016-09-16|access-date=2016-09-16}}\n\nA third title fight, a [[UFC Women's Strawweight Championship]] bout between the champion [[Joanna Jędrzejczyk]] and [[Karolina Kowalkiewicz]] was also part of the card.{{cite web|url=http://fansided.com/2016/09/23/joanna-jedrzejczyk-vs-karolina-kowalkiewicz-ufc-205/|title=Report: Joanna Jędrzejczyk to defend title against Karolina Kowalkiewicz at UFC 205|publisher=fansided.com|author=José Youngs|date=2016-09-23|access-date=2016-09-23}} The duo met previously in their amateur days in 2012, when Jędrzejczyk defeated Kowalkiewicz via submission in the Amatorska Liga MMA Tournament final.{{cite web|url=https://www.flocombat.com/article/46513-joanna-jedrzejczyk-vs-karolina-kowalkiewicz-set-for-ufc-205|title=Joanna Jędrzejczyk vs. Karolina Kowalkiewicz set for UFC 205|publisher=flocombat.com|author=Jim Edwards|date=2016-09-23|access-date=2016-09-23}}\n\n[[File:Madison Square Garden, February 2013.jpg|thumb|300px|[[Madison Square Garden]] hosted the much anticipated [[New York City]] debut of the UFC.]]\n\nA potential middleweight title eliminator between former [[UFC Middleweight Championship|UFC Middleweight Champion]] [[Chris Weidman]] and [[Wrestling at the 2000 Summer Olympics|2000 Olympic silver medalist]] and former world champion in [[freestyle wrestling]] [[Yoel Romero]] took place at this portion of the event.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/ex-champ-chris-weidman-vs-yoel-romero-slated-for-ufc-205-in-new-york-city|title=Ex-champ Chris Weidman vs. Yoel Romero slated for UFC 205 in New York City|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=John Morgan and Dann Stupp|date=2016-09-15|access-date=2016-09-15}}\n\nA welterweight bout between former champion [[Robbie Lawler]] and former lightweight title challenger [[Donald Cerrone]] was the first confirmed bout of the event.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/robbie-lawler-vs-donald-cerrone-a-go-for-ufc-205-dana-white-says|title=Robbie Lawler vs. Donald Cerrone a go for UFC 205, Dana White says|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Steven Marrocco|date=2016-09-07|access-date=2016-09-07}} However, just a few days after the announcement, it was revealed the Lawler decided to take a little more time to get ready for the fight after losing his title via knockout at [[UFC 201]].{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2016/9/10/12871178/robbie-lawler-vs-donald-cerrone-scratched-from-ufc-205|title=Robbie Lawler vs. Donald Cerrone scratched from UFC 205|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Ariel Helwani|date=2016-09-10|access-date=2016-09-10}} He was replaced by [[The Ultimate Fighter: Team Jones vs. Team Sonnen]] middleweight winner [[Kelvin Gastelum]], who was scheduled to face [[Jorge Masvidal]] one week earlier than the event.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2016/9/14/12920808/donald-cerrone-vs-kelvin-gastelum-in-the-works-for-ufc-205|title=Donald Cerrone vs. Kelvin Gastelum in the works for UFC 205|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Ariel Helwani|date=2016-09-14|access-date=2016-09-14}}\n\nOpening the main card was a women's bantamweight bout between former [[Strikeforce Women's Bantamweight Championship|Strikeforce]] and [[UFC Women's Bantamweight Championship|UFC Women's Bantamweight Champion]] [[Miesha Tate]] and [[Raquel Pennington]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/miesha-tate-vs-raquel-pennington-slated-for-ufc-205-in-new-york-city|title=Miesha Tate vs. Raquel Pennington slated for UFC 205 in New York City|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-09-23|access-date=2016-09-23}}{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/10/broadcast-plans-set-for-ufc-205-and-it-is-a-thing-of-beauty|title=Full [[Frankie Edgar]] and [[Jeremy Stephens]] was the featured bout of the preliminary card.{{cite web|url=https://www.ufc.com/news/UFC-Announces-Five-Bouts-for-NYC-Event|title=UFC announces five bouts for NYC event|publisher=ufc.com|author=Thomas Gerbasi|date=2016-09-21|access-date=2016-09-21}} A few other bouts also took part of the [[Fox Sports 1]] televised prelims: \n*Despite being originally expected to face Alvarez, [[Khabib Nurmagomedov]] faced [[Michael Johnson (fighter)|Michael Johnson]] instead in a potential lightweight title eliminator.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/michael-johnson-vs-khabib-nurmagomedov-official-for-ufc-205-in-new-york|title=Michael Johnson vs. Khabib Nurmagomedov official for UFC 205 in New Bellator champions#Welterweight Championship|Bellator Welterweight Champion]] [[Lyman Good]] and [[Belal Muhammad]] was scheduled for this portion of the event.{{cite web|url=https://www.newsday.com/sports/mixed-martial-arts/lyman-good-to-face-belal-muhammad-at-ufc-205-s42049|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160924101530/http://www.newsday.com/sports/mixed-martial-arts/lyman-good-to-face-belal-muhammad-at-ufc-205-1.12357132|url-status=live|archive-date=September 24, 2016|title=Lyman Good to face Belal Muhammad at UFC 205|publisher=newsday.com|author=Marc La Monica|date=2016-09-23|access-date=2016-09-23}} However, on October 24, Good was pulled from the card after being notified by [[USADA]] due to a potential anti-doping violation stemming from an out-of-competition sample collected ten days earlier.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/10/lyman-good-receives-usada-notice-pulled-from-ufc-205|title=Lyman Good receives USADA notice, pulled from UFC 205|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-10-24|access-date=2016-10-24}} He was replaced by [[Vicente Luque]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/10/vicente-luque-replaces-lyman-good-at-ufc-205-in-new-york-city05|title=Vicente Luque replaces Lyman Good at UFC 205 in New York City|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-10-25|access-date=2016-10-25}}{{Dead link|date=July 2025 |bot=InternetArchiveBot |fix-attempted=yes }}\n\nA lightweight bout between [[Al Iaquinta]] and former welterweight title challenger [[Thiago Alves (fighter)|Thiago Alves]] was previously linked to [[UFC 202]].{{cite web|url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2016/5/23/11744954/report-alves-vs-iaquinta-set-for-ufc-202-in-august-mma-news|title=Alves vs. Iaquinta set for UFC 202 in August|publisher=bloodyelbow.com|author=Lucas Rezende|date=2016-05-23|access-date=2016-07-31|archive-date=2016-09-30|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160930220024/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2016/5/23/11744954/report-alves-vs-iaquinta-set-for-ufc-202-in-august-mma-news|url-status=dead}} However, the pairing was initially delayed and was expected to be contested at this event.{{cite web|url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/news/Thiago-Alves-Moves-to-155-Pounds-Meets-Al-Iaquinta-at-UFC-205-in-New-York-110761|title=Thiago Alves moves to 155 pounds, meets Al Iaquinta at UFC 205 in New York|publisher=sherdog.com|author=Tristen Critchfield|date=2016-09-06|access-date=2016-09-06}} Subsequently, Iaquinta announced on September 19 that he pulled out due to a contract dispute with the promotion.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2016/9/19/12981522/al-iaquinta-willing-to-walk-away-from-mma-if-dispute-with-ufc-remains|title=Al Iaquinta willing to walk away from MMA if dispute with UFC remains unsettled|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Shaun Al-Shatti|date=2016-09-19|access-date=2016-09-19}} He was replaced by [[Jim Miller (fighter)|Jim Miller]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/report-jim-miller-fills-al-iaquintas-spot-at-ufc-205-takes-on-thiago-alves|title=Report: Jim Miller fills Al Iaquinta's spot at UFC 205, takes on Thiago Alves|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-09-19|access-date=2016-09-19}} The bout headlined the [[UFC Fight Pass]] portion of the event.\n\nTo complete the card, there was a women's bantamweight bout between former title challenger [[Liz Carmouche]] and [[Katlyn Chookagian]].{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2016/9/15/12926520/liz-carmouche-vs-katlyn-chookagian-slated-for-ufc-205|title=Liz Carmouche vs. Katlyn Chookagian slated for UFC 205|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Ariel Helwani|date=2016-09-15|access-date=2016-09-15}}\n\nA light heavyweight bout between [[Gian Villante]] and [[Marcos Rogério de Lima]] was briefly linked to the event.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2016/9/8/12849102/gian-villante-vs-marcos-rogerio-de-lima-added-to-ufc-205|title=Gian Villante vs. Marcos Rogerio de Lima added to UFC 205|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Guilherme Cruz|date=2016-09-08|access-date=2016-09-08}} However, Villante pulled out of the fight on September 21 citing injury and the bout was scrapped.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2016/9/21/13001276/gian-villante-vs-marcos-rogerio-de-lima-off-ufc-205|title=Gian Villante vs. Marcos Rogerio de Lima off UFC 205|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Ariel Helwani|date=2016-09-21|access-date=2016-09-21}}\n\nA middleweight bout between [[Tim Kennedy (fighter)|Tim Kennedy]] and former [[UFC Light Heavyweight Championship|UFC Light Heavyweight Champion]] and divisional newcomer [[Rashad Evans]] was scheduled for the FS1 portion of the event.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2016/9/9/12863916/rashad-evans-vs-tim-kennedy-slated-for-ufc-205|title=Rashad Evans vs. Tim Kennedy slated for UFC 205|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Ariel Helwani|date=2016-09-09|access-date=2016-09-09}} However, on November 8, Evans was pulled from the fight after an undisclosed irregularity was found during his pre-fight medical exam. Subsequently, Kennedy was removed from the card as well.{{cite web|url=http://fansided.com/2016/11/08/rashad-evans-off-ufc-205/|title=Rashad Evans pulled from UFC 205 fight against Tim Kennedy|publisher=fansided.com|author=Jose Youngs|date=2016-11-08|access-date=2016-11-08}}{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2016/11/8/13567552/rashad-evans-vs-tim-kennedy-fight-scratched-from-ufc-205|title=Rashad Evans vs. Tim Kennedy fight scratched from UFC 205|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Ariel Helwani|date=2016-11-08|access-date=2016-11-08}}{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/11/rashad-evans-vs-tim-kennedy-rescheduled-for-ufc-206-in-toronto|title=Rashad Evans vs. Tim Kennedy rescheduled for UFC 206 in Toronto|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Mike Bohn|date=2016-11-09|access-date=2016-11-09}}\n\n===Weigh-ins===\nAt the weigh-ins, Alves was over his division weight limit by six pounds, weighing in at 162.6 lb. Per [[New York State Athletic Commission]] (NYSAC) rules, opposing lightweight fighters must weigh within 7 pounds of one another. Having not yet completed his own official weigh-in, Alves' opponent Jim Miller (who was within the designated lightweight limit) was able to rehydrate and officially weigh in over the limit himself, at 157.6 lb, allowing the bout to proceed as catchweight. NYSAC and UFC officials indicated that Alves must not weigh more than 173 lb the day of the fight, or it would be cancelled. As a result of missing the original designated weight, Alves was fined 20% of his purse, which went to his opponent.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/11/ufc-205-early-weigh-in-results-and-live-video-stream-9-a-m-et-1#undefined.uxfs|title=UFC 205 early weigh-in results: Thiago Alves and Kelvin Gastelum force last-minute scramble|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-11-11|access-date=2016-11-11}}\n\nKelvin Gastelum did not appear for the official weigh-ins, announcing via Twitter that he would not fight Cerrone \"on account that he couldn't make the weight limit\". Prior to this announcement, Cerrone had already successfully weighed in at 170.4 lb, meaning he was unable to subsequently rehydrate as Miller did, to keep within 7 pounds of his over-weight opponent.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/11/kelvin-gastelum-announces-ufc-205-fight-with-donald-cerrone-is-off|title=Kelvin Gastelum announces UFC 205 fight with Donald Cerrone is off|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-11-11|access-date=2016-11-11}}{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/11/ufc-205-main-card-down-to-5-bouts-due-to-kelvin-gastelums-latest-weight-blunder|title=UFC 205 main card down to 5 bouts due to Kelvin Gastelum's latest weight blunder|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-11-11|access-date=2016-11-11}} In an interview after hearing the news, Cerrone stated he would have \"blown weight too,\" had Gastelum informed him prior.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2016/11/11/13599870/donald-cerrone-reacts-to-being-pulled-from-ufc-205|title=Donald Cerrone reacts to being pulled from UFC 205|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Ariel Helwani|date=2016-11-11|access-date=2016-11-17}} As a result, Gastelum was pulled from the card and UFC president [[Dana White]] stated he will never let him fight at welterweight in the organization again, and that he believes Gastelum missed weight by 10 pounds.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/11/dana-white-says-kelvin-gastelum-done-at-170-after-missing-weight-for-ufc-205|title=Dana White says Kelvin Gastelum done at 170 after missing weight for UFC 205|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Mike Bohn|date=2016-11-11|access-date=2016-11-11}} Despite the one-day notice, it was reported a small number of fighters were interested in filling in to fight Cerrone. According to one candidate [[Alan Jouban]], UFC matchmaker Joe Silva was interested in having him fill in, however, according to Jouban, the NYSAC would not consider sanctioning any fighters on such short notice.{{cite web|url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2016/11/12/13604128/ufc-205-cerrone-jouban-joe-silva-gastelum-nysac-mma-news|title=Jouban: Joe Silva 'loved' Cerrone fight, but NYSAC wouldn't sanction it|publisher=bloodyelbow.com|author=Nick Baldwin|date=2016-11-12|access-date=2016-11-17|archive-date=2016-11-16|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20161116233703/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2016/11/12/13604128/ufc-205-cerrone-jouban-joe-silva-gastelum-nysac-mma-news|url-status=dead}}\n\nConsequently, the bout was scrapped. Cerrone was quickly rescheduled and faced [[Matt Brown (fighter)|Matt Brown]] the following month at [[UFC 206]].{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2016/11/11/13601562/donald-cerrone-vs-matt-brown-slated-for-ufc-206|title=Donald Cerrone vs. Matt Brown slated for UFC 206|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Dave Doyle|date=2016-11-11|access-date=2016-11-11}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card}}\n{{MMAevent bout|Lightweight|[[Conor McGregor]]|def.|[[Eddie Alvarez]] (c)|TKO of the Night: Tyron Woodley vs. Stephen Thompson'''\n*'''Performance of the Night:''' '''Conor McGregor''' and '''Yoel Romero'''\n\n==Records set==\nThe ceremonial [[weigh-in]]s set an attendance record of 15,480 spectators.{{Cite web|url=http://www.foxsports.com/ufc/story/here-are-all-the-records-ufc-205-alvarez-vs-mcgregor-shattered-on-saturday-111516|title=Here are all the records UFC 205: Alvarez vs. McGregor shattered on Saturday|first=2016 at 1:26p|last=ET|date=15 November 2016|website=FOX Sports|access-date=10 January 2020}} The event reportedly generated $17,700,000 in [[gate receipts]], which broke the [[UFC 129]] record for a mixed martial arts event, as well as the record for the venue, [[Madison Square Garden]]. The attendance of 20,427 was the highest for a domestic event.{{failed verification|date=November 2016}} These records have since been surpassed by [[UFC 306]] on September 14, 2024 which generated $21,829,245{{Cite web|url=https://www.wrestling-online.com/wwe/wwe-contingent-at-ufc-306-at-the-sphere-in-las-vegas/|title=WWE contingent at UFC 306 at The Sphere in Las Vegas|publisher=wrestling-online.com|author=Colin Vassallo|date=2024-09-15|access-date=2024-09-15}} and [[UFC 281]] on November 12, 2022 which had 20,845 people in attendance.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/lists/ufc-281-post-event-facts-alex-pereira-makes-history-title-win-brock-lesnar|title=UFC 281 post-event facts: Alex Pereira makes history with title win in 8th career fight|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Mike Bohn|date=2022-11-13|access-date=2022-11-13}}\n\nThe pre-fight documentary ''Fighting for History'' drew 2.4 million viewers on [[Fox Broadcasting Company|Fox]], the most of any preview show in UFC history.{{cite web|url=http://www.foxsports.com/ufc/story/here-are-all-the-records-ufc-205-alvarez-vs-mcgregor-shattered-on-saturday-111516|title=Here are all the records UFC 205: Alvarez vs. McGregor shattered on Saturday|work=foxsports.com|author=Damon Martin|date=2016-11-15|access-date=2016-11-17}}\n\nThe [[Reebok]] athlete outfitting pay was the highest in history, with a total of $392,500.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/11/ufc-205-athlete-outfitting-pay-champs-mcgregor-woodley-jedrzejczyk-lead-record-setting-payout|title=UFC 205 Athlete Outfitting pay: Champs McGregor, Woodley, Jedrzejczyk lead record-setting payout|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-11-13|access-date=2016-11-13}}\n\nWith his win, McGregor became the first fighter in the promotion's history to hold two titles simultaneously.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/11/ufc-205-results-conor-mcgregor-dominates-eddie-alvarez-claims-history-as-2-division-champ-1|title=Conor McGregor dominates Eddie Alvarez, claims history as 2-division champ at UFC 205|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Steven Marrocco|date=2016-11-13|access-date=2016-11-13}}\n\n==Aftermath==\nOn November 21, the NYSAC administratively suspended Gastelum for six months because he did not attend the weigh-ins. An administrative suspension is different from a typical commission suspension in that it does not have to be reciprocated by other jurisdictions.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2016/11/21/13709502/nysac-gives-kelvin-gastelum-six-month-administrative-suspension-for|title=NYSAC gives Kelvin Gastelum six-month administrative suspension for missing weigh-ins|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Marc Raimondi|date=2016-11-21|access-date=2016-11-21}} Despite being originally expected to value only in New York, it was announced two days later that the suspension was added to the database of the [[Association of Boxing Commissions]] (ABC) and it could be extend to other jurisdictions as well. Gastelum's team announced they would enter an appeal.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2016/11/22/13718908/kelvin-gastelum-appealing-nysac-suspension|title=Kelvin Gastelum appealing NYSAC suspension|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Marc Raimondi|date=2016-11-22|access-date=2016-11-23}} Gastelum was cleared of his suspension on November 30, which allowed him to face Kennedy at [[UFC 206]], as Evans once again was pulled from the bout.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2016/11/29/13788382/kelvin-gastelum-free-of-suspension-will-face-tim-kennedy-at-ufc-206|title=Kelvin Gastelum free of suspension, will face Tim Kennedy at UFC 206|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Marc Raimondi|date=2016-11-30|access-date=2016-11-30}}\n\nThe NYSAC also issued a three-month suspension for Alves due to him missing weight,{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2016/11/22/13719770/thiago-alves-suspended-three-months-for-missing-weight-at-ufc-205|title=Thiago Alves suspended three months for missing weight at UFC 205, will continue at lightweight|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Guilherme Cruz|date=2016-11-22|access-date=2016-11-22}} as well as a two-month suspension for Romero for leaving cage right after defeating Weidman. Romero jumped over the Octagon in adulation, did a soldier's march around the outside perimeter of the cage, and then entered back in for the official results.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2016/11/23/13732868/yoel-romero-suspended-60-days-by-nysac-for-leaving-cage-after-beating|title=Yoel Romero suspended 60 days by NYSAC for leaving cage after beating Chris Weidman|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Marc Raimondi|date=2016-11-23|access-date=2016-11-24}}\n\nOn November 26, due to his inactivity in the featherweight division, McGregor was stripped of the [[UFC Featherweight Championship]], therefore promoting [[José Aldo]] to undisputed champion.{{cite web|url=https://www.ufc.com/news/McGregor-vacates-featherweight-title|title=McGregor vacates featherweight title; Aldo new undisputed champion |author=Staff|publisher=ufc.com|date=2016-11-26|access-date=2016-11-26}}{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/11/stripping-conor-mcgregor-of-his-ufc-featherweight-title-makes-some-sense-but-not-like-this|title=Stripping Conor McGregor of his UFC featherweight title makes sense – but not like this|author=Ben Fowlkes|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=2016-11-27|access-date=2016-11-27}} \n\nOn April 1, 2017, it was announced that Good will receive a six-month suspension from USADA retroactive to his positive test. \"USADA found that Lyman's October 2016 flagged urine specimen was the result of tainted vitamins and not the result of any intentional violation\", Good’s lawyer David Fish said.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2017/4/1/15150256/ufcs-lyman-good-to-get-six-month-usada-suspension-for-tainted-product|title=UFC's Lyman Good to get six-month USADA suspension for tainted product|author=Marc Raimondi|publisher=mmafighting.com|date=2017-04-01|access-date=2017-04-01}}\n\n==See also==\n*[[List of UFC events]]\n*[[2016 in UFC]]\n\n==References==\n{{Reflist|30em}}\n{{UFC Events}}\n\n[[Category:Ultimate Fighting Championship flagship events]]\n[[Category:2016 in mixed martial arts]]\n[[Category:2016 in sports in New York 3","revisions":[{"slots":{"main":{"contentmodel":"wikitext","contentformat":"text/x-wiki","content":"{{short description|UFC mixed martial arts television series and event in 2016}}\n{{Infobox television\n|image = \n|genre = [[reality television|Reality]], [[Broadcasting of sports events|Sports]]\n|runtime = 60 minutes\n|creator = [[Frank Fertitta III]], [[Lorenzo Fertitta]], [[Dana America 2]]\n}}\n\t\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter: Latin America 3''''' is an installment of the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC)-produced [[reality television]] series ''[[The Ultimate Fighter]]''.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/05/cast-revealed-for-tuf-latin-america-3-with-coaches-forrest-griffin-and-chuck-liddell|title=Cast revealed for 'TUF: Latin America 3' with coaches Forrest Griffin and Chuck Liddell|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-05-31|access-date=2016-05-31}}{{cite revealed|publisher=ufc.com|author=Staff|date=2016-05-31|access-date=2016-05-31}}\n\nOn May 31, 2016, the UFC announced that the 16 competitors for the season would be made up of [[Lightweight (MMA)|lightweight]] fighters from various locations around [[Latin America]].\n\nThe cast consisted of fighters from 9 countries: Bedoya, from [[Colombia]], Castillo, from [[Nicaragua]], Cárdenas and Villaseca, from [[Chile]], Chalo, from [[Venezuela]], Flores, from [[Bolivia]], Puelles, from [[Peru]], Ganin and Rojo, from [[Argentina]], Zamora, from [[Costa Rica]] and Bravo, Martínez, Quintanar, Rodríguez, Sabori and Villegas, from [[Mexico]].\n\nThe coaches for the season are former [[UFC Light Heavyweight Championship|UFC Light Heavyweight champions]] [[Forrest Griffin]] and [[Chuck Liddell]]. Both retired fighters have been coaches on previous ''The Ultimate Fighter'' installments.\n\nFilming for the season began on May 16 in [[Buenos Aires]], Argentina.\n\n==Cast==\n\n===Teams===\n'''Team {{center|(T)KO}}\n|  \n| (Technical) Knock Out\n|-\n|}\n{{Clear}}\n\n==The Ultimate Fighter Latin America 3 Finale: dos Anjos vs. Ferguson==\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name=The Ultimate Fighter Latin America 3 Finale: dos Anjos vs. Ferguson\n|image=TUF_LA_3_Finale.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date={{Start date|2016|11|05}}\n|venue=[[Mexico City Arena|Arena Ciudad de México]]\n|city= [[Mexico City]], Mexico\n|attendance=11,460{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/11/ufc-fight-night-98-draws-announced-attendance-of-11460-in-mexico-city|title=UFC Fight Night 98 draws announced attendance of 11,460 in Mexico City|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-11-06|access-date=2016-11-06}}\n|gate=\n|previousevent=[[UFC Fight Night: Lamas vs. Penn]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC 205|UFC 205: Alvarez vs. McGregor]]\n}}\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter Latin America 3 Finale: dos Anjos vs. Ferguson''''' (also known as '''''UFC Fight Night 98''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held on November 5, 2016, at [[Mexico City Arena|Arena Ciudad de México]] in [[Mexico City]], Mexico{{cite news|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/chuck-liddell-forrest-griffin-to-coach-season-3-the-ultimate-fighter-latin-america|title=Legends Liddell, Griffin to coach TUF LATAM|publisher=ufc.com|author=Staff|date=2016-04-11|access-date=2016-04-11}}{{cite news|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/Dos-Anjos-vs-Ferguson-to-Headline-in-Mexico-City|title=Dos Anjos vs. Ferguson to headline in Mexico City|publisher=ufc.com|author=Thomas Gerbasi|date=2016-08-03|access-date=2016-08-03}}\n\n===Background===\nA potential lightweight title eliminator bout between former [[UFC Lightweight Championship|UFC Lightweight Champion]] [[Rafael dos Anjos]] and [[The Ultimate Fighter: Team Lesnar vs. Team dos Santos]] winner [[Tony Ferguson]] headlined the event.{{cite news|url=https://champions.co/posts/4019663|title=Rafael dos Anjos vs. Tony Ferguson confirmed to headline Mexico City|publisher=champions.co|author=Amy Kaplan|date=2016-08-02|access-date=2016-08-02}}{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/08/rafael-dos-anjos-meets-tony-ferguson-in-november-ufc-card-in-mexico-city|title=Rafael dos Anjos meets Tony Ferguson on November UFC card in Mexico City|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-08-02|access-date=2016-08-02}}\n\nThe lightweight finale of The Ultimate Fighter: Latin America 3 was contested at the event.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2016/10/27/13438804/claudio-puelles-fights-martin-bravo-in-the-tuf-latin-america-3-final|title=Claudio Puelles fights Martin Bravo in the TUF: Latin America 3 Final|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Danny Segura|access-date=2016-10-27|date=2016-10-27}}\n\n[[The Ultimate Fighter: Team Jones vs. Team Sonnen]] middleweight winner [[Kelvin Gastelum]] was expected to face [[Jorge Masvidal]] in a welterweight bout at the event. However, on September 14, Gastelum was removed in favor of a replacement bout against former lightweight title challenger [[Donald Cerrone]] at [[UFC 205]], one week later.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2016/9/14/12920808/donald-cerrone-vs-kelvin-gastelum-in-the-works-for-ufc-205|title=Donald Cerrone vs. Kelvin Gastelum in the works for UFC 205|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Ariel Helwani|date=2016-09-14|access-date=2016-09-14}} Subsequently, Masvidal was removed from the card entirely rescheduled to face [[Jake Ellenberger]] the following month at [[The Ultimate Fighter: Tournament of Champions#The Ultimate Fighter 24 Finale|The Ultimate Fighter: Tournament of Champions Finale]].{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/jake-ellenberger-vs-jorge-masvidal-booked-for-decembers-tuf-24-finale-in-las-vegas|title=Jake Ellenberger vs. Jorge Masvidal booked for December's TUF 24 Finale in Las Vegas|publisher=mmajunkie.com |author=Staff|access-date=2016-09-28|date=2016-09-28}}\n\nAs a result of the cancellation of [[UFC Fight Night: Lamas vs. Penn]], a bout between [[Sam Alvey]] and [[Alex Nicholson (fighter)|Alex Nicholson]] was rescheduled for this event.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/10/sam-alvey-vs-alex-nicholson-moves-to-ufc-fight-night-98-in-mexico|title=Sam Alvey vs. Alex Nicholson moves to UFC Fight Night 98 in Mexico|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|access-date=2016-10-11|date=2016-10-11}}\n\n[[Guido Cannetti]] was expected to face [[Marco Beltrán]] in a rematch at the event. The pairing first met at one of [[The Ultimate Fighter: Latin America]]'s tournament bouts where Beltrán won a controversial decision. However, on October 29, Cannetti was pulled from the bout after [[USADA]] revealed a potential anti-doping violation from a sample taken October 15.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2016/10/29/13465852/guido-cannetti-flagged-by-usada-out-of-tuf-latin-america-3-finale|title=Guido Cannetti flagged by USADA, out of TUF: Latin America 3 Finale fight with Marco Beltran|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Danny Segura|access-date=2016-10-29|date=2016-10-29}} He was replaced by former [[List of Bellator champions#Featherweight Championship|Bellator Featherweight Champion]] and one time [[UFC Bantamweight Championship]] challenger [[Joe Soto]] and the bout was contested at a catchweight of 140 lb.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2016/10/31/13484352/ufc-fight-night-98-fight-card-joe-soto-replaces-guido-cannetti-against-marco-beltran|title=Joe Soto replaces Guido Cannetti, against Marco Beltran at UFC Fight Night 98|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Shaun Al-Shatti|access-date=2016-11-01|date=2016-11-01}}\n\nAt the event's weigh-ins, [[Charles Oliveira]] failed to make the featherweight limit for his fight with [[Ricardo Lamas]], coming in nine pounds over the 146 lb weight allowance. He was fined 30 percent of his earnings, and Lamas and promotion officials insisted that Oliveira not weigh more than 160 lb the day of the fight or the fight would be cancelled.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/11/ufc-fight-night-98-weigh-in-results-charles-oliveira-badly-misses-weight-but-fight-still-on|title=UFC Fight Night 98 weigh-in results: Charles Oliveira badly misses weight, but fight still on|publisher=MMAJunkie.com|access-date=2016-11-04|date=2016-11-04}}{{cite web|url=http://www.sportsjoe.ie/mma/ufc-star-charles-oliveira-misses-weight-biggest-margin-yet/101668|title=UFC star Charles Oliveira misses weight by his biggest margin yet|publisher=sportsjoe.ie|author=Ben Kiely|access-date=2016-11-04|date=2016-11-04}} [[Felipe Arantes]] also missed weight for his bout against [[Erik Pérez]], coming in two pounds over the bantamweight weight allowance. He was fined 20 percent of his purse.\n\n===Results===\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (Fox Sports 1)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Lightweight\n|[[Tony Ferguson]]\n|def.\n|[[Rafael dos web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/11/ufc-fight-night-98-bonuses-an-emotional-choke-a-spinning-backfist-and-a-five-round-slugfest|title=UFC Fight Night 98 bonuses: An emotional choke, a spinning backfist and a five-round slugfest|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-11-06|access-date=2016-11-06}}\n*'''Fight of the Night: Tony Ferguson vs. Rafael dos Anjos'''\n*'''Performance of the Night:''' '''Ricardo Lamas''' and '''Douglas Silva de Andrade'''\n\n===Aftermath===\nOn August 21, 2017, it was announced that Cannetti was given a 10-month suspension by USADA, retroactive to October 28, 2016. The provisional suspension came after testing positive for [[ostarine]], [[hydrochlorothiazide]] and [[chlorothiazide]]. USADA conducted tests on the supplements Cannetti was taking at the time of the sample collection, and concluded that the supplements were tainted.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2017/8/19/16171782/guido-cannetti-accepts-10-month-usada-suspension-after-taking-tainted-supplement|title=Guido Cannetti accepts 10-month USADA suspension after taking tainted supplement|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Danny Segura|date=2017-08-19|access-date=2017-08-21}}\n\n==See also==\n*[[The Ultimate Fighter]]\n*[[List of UFC events]]\n*[[2016 in event in 2016}}\n{{Use dmy dates|date=April 2022}}\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name=UFC 204: Bisping vs. Henderson 2|promotion=Ultimate Fighting Championship\n|date={{start news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/10/ufc-204-draws-crowd-in-excess-of-16000-fans-for-1-96-million-live-gate-in-manchester|title=UFC 204 draws crowd in excess of 16,000 fans for $1.96 million live |title=Archived copy |access-date=11 August 2017 |archive-date=27 June 2011 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20110627142203/http://mmapayout.com/blue-book/pay-per-view/ |url-status=dead }}\n|previousevent=[[UFC Fight Night: Lineker vs. Dodson]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC Fight Night: Lamas vs. Penn]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC 204: Bisping vs. Henderson 2''''' was a [[mixed martial arts]] event produced by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] held on October 8, 2016, at the [[Manchester Arena]] in [[Manchester]], [[England]].{{cite news|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2016/8/4/12383476/michael-bisping-vs-dan-henderson-2-agreed-for-ufc-204|title=Michael Bisping vs. Dan Henderson 2 agreed for UFC 204|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Ariel Helwani|date=2016-08-04|access-date=2016-08-04}}{{cite news|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/Bisping-to-Defend-UFC-Title-on-Home-Turf|title=Bisping to defend UFC title on home turf|publisher=ufc.com|author=UFC Press Release|date=2016-08-15|access-date=2016-08-15}}\n\n==Background==\nThis was the fourth UFC event contested in Manchester and first [[pay per view|PPV]] event held in the European/Middle East market since [[UFC 112]].\n\nIn order for the event to be broadcast live during [[prime time]] hours on the east coast of North America, the main card was expected to begin at 3:00 am (October 9) local time in Manchester [[GMT]], with a full preliminary card beginning at approximately 11:00 pm (October 8) local time.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/08/ufc-204-bisping-vs-henderson-2-gets-7-bouts-3-a-m-local-start-time-for-main-card|title='UFC 204: Bisping vs. Henderson 2' gets 7 bouts, 3 a.m. local start time for main card|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-08-15|access-date=2016-08-15}} Despite the very late starting time, ticket demand was exceptionally high, as all tickets for the venue sold out in a matter of minutes.{{cite news|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/manchester-unites-despite-3-a-m-main-card-in-england-ufc-204-sells-out-in-6-minutes|title=Manchester unites: Despite 3 a.m. main card in England, UFC 204 sells out in 6 minutes.|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-09-09|access-date=2016-09-09}}\n\nThe event was headlined by a [[UFC Middleweight Championship]] bout between current champion [[Michael Bisping]] and the former [[List of Pride champions#Welterweight championship|Pride Welterweight]], [[List of Pride champions#Middleweight championship|Pride Middleweight]] and former [[List of Strikeforce champions#Light Heavyweight Championship|Strikeforce Light Heavyweight Champion]] [[Dan Henderson]].{{Cite web|url=http://www.foxsports.com/ufc/story/michael-bisping-announces-rematch-with-dan-henderson-at-ufc-204-in-england-080516|title=Michael Bisping announces rematch with Dan Henderson at UFC 204 in England|work=foxsports.com|author=Damon Martin|date=2016-08-05|access-date=2016-08-05}} The pairing met previously in July 2009 at [[UFC 100]] with Henderson taking the victory via second round highlight reel knockout.{{Cite web|url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2009/7/11/945539/ufc-100-results-dan-henderson|title=UFC 100 results: Dan Henderson crushes Michael Bisping|publisher=bloodyelbow|author=Nate Wilcox|date=2009-07-11|access-date=2016-09-18|archive-date=17 May 2018|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180517153628/https://www.bloodyelbow.com/2009/7/11/945539/ufc-100-results-dan-henderson|url-status=dead}}\n\nA bantamweight bout between [[Brad Pickett]] and [[Iuri Alcântara]] was previously linked to [[UFC Fight Night: Arlovski vs. Barnett]].{{Cite web|url=http://sportv.globo.com/site/combate/noticia/2016/08/iuri-marajo-substitui-briones-e-encara-brad-pickett-no-ufc-da-alemanha.html|title=Iuri Marajo replaces Briones and sees Brad Pickett at UFC in Germany (Brazilian Portuguese)|publisher=sportv.globo.com|author=Raphael Marinho|date=2016-08-02|access-date=2016-08-11}} However, it was contested at this event.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2016/8/9/12419480/brad-pickett-vs-iuri-alcantara-scheduled-for-ufc-204-in-manchester|title=Brad Pickett vs. Iuri Alcantara scheduled for UFC 204|author=Danny Segura|publisher=mmafighting.com|date=2016-08-10|access-date=2016-08-11}}\n\n[[Ruslan Magomedov]] was expected to face [[Stefan Struve]] at this event, but pulled out on September 9 due to a [[staph infection]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/infection-forces-ruslan-magomedov-out-of-ufc-204-bout-with-stefan-struve|title=Infection forces Ruslan Magomedov out of UFC 204 bout with Stefan Struve|author=Dann Stupp|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=2016-09-09|access-date=2016-09-09}} Struve faced [[Daniel Omielańczuk]]{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/daniel-omielanczuk-steps-in-as-replacement-faces-stefan-struve-at-ufc-204|title=Daniel Omielanczuk steps in as replacement, faces Stefan Struve at UFC 204|author=Steven Marrocco|publisher=mmajunkie.com|date=2016-09-14|access-date=2016-09-14}}\n\n[[Arnold Allen (fighter)|Arnold Allen]] was expected to face [[Mirsad Bektic|Mirsad Bektić]] at the event. However, Allen pulled out of the fight on 29 September for undisclosed reasons.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/arnold-allen-out-of-ufc-204-fight-with-mirsad-bektic-promotion-seeking-featherweight-replacement|title=Arnold Allen out of UFC 204 fight with Mirsad Bektic, promotion seeking featherweight replacement|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-09-29|access-date=2016-09-29}} [[Jeremy Kennedy]] was briefly linked as the replacement.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/10/jeremy-kennedy-replaces-arnold-allen-in-next-weeks-ufc-204-main-card-opener|title=Jeremy Kennedy replaces Arnold Allen in next week's UFC 204 main-card opener|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-10-01|access-date=2016-10-01}} Subsequently, just hours after his participation was publicly confirmed, Kennedy indicated he was injured and unable to fight.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/10/replacement-jeremy-kennedy-injured-yet-another-replacement-needed-for-next-weeks-ufc-204|title=Replacement Jeremy Kennedy injured; yet another replacement needed for next week's UFC 204|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Dann Stupp and John Morgan|date=2016-10-01|access-date=2016-10-01}} Bektić faced [[Russell Doane]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/10/russell-doane-steps-in-to-face-mirsad-bektic-on-five-days-notice-at-ufc-204|title=Russell Doane steps in to face Mirsad Bektic on five days' notice at UFC 204|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-10-04|access-date=2016-10-04}}\n\n[[Reza Madadi]] was expected to face promotional newcomer [[Marc Diakiese]] at the event. However, Madadi pulled out of the fight in the week leading up to the event citing an eye injury and was replaced by Łukasz Sajewski.{{Cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/2016/10/lukasz-sajewski-replaces-reza-madadi-at-saturdays-ufc-204|title=Lukasz Sajewski replaces Reza Madadi at Saturday's UFC 204|date=2 October 2016|access-date=13 January 2020}}\n\n[[Ian Entwistle]] was expected to face [[Rob Font]] at the event. However, on the day prior to the event, Entwistle fell ill during the [[weight cutting]] process and the bout was scrapped.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/10/ian-entwistle-off-saturdays-ufc-204-card-diakiese-sajewski-promoted-to-fs1-prelims|title=Ian Entwistle off Saturday's UFC 204 card; Diakiese-Sajewski promoted to Länsberg]]\n|followingevent=[[UFC 204|UFC 204: Bisping vs. Henderson 2]]\n}}\n'''''UFC Fight Night: Lineker vs. Dodson''''' (also known as '''''UFC Fight Night 96''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event produced by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] that was held on October 1, 2016, at [[Moda Center]] in [[Portland, Oregon]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/UFC-Heads-to-Oregon-in-October|title=UFC heads to Oregon in October |publisher=ufc.com|author=Thomas Gerbasi|date=2016-07-23|access-date=2016-07-23}}{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/07/ufc-returns-to-portland-after-more-than-seven-years-with-ufc-fight-night-on-oct-1|title=UFC returns to Portland after more than seven years with UFC Fight Night on Oct. 1|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-07-23|access-date=2016-07-23}}\n\n==Background==\nThe event was the second that the organization hosted in Portland, with the first being [[UFC 102]] in August 2009.\n\nThe event was headlined by a bantamweight bout between [[John Lineker]] and former two-time [[UFC Flyweight Championship]] challenger and [[The Ultimate Fighter: Team Bisping vs. Team Miller]] bantamweight winner [[John Dodson (fighter)|John Dodson]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/08/john-dodson-meets-john-lineker-in-ufc-fight-night-96-main-event|title=John Dodson meets John Lineker in UFC Fight Night 96 main event|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-08-13|access-date=2016-08-13}}\n\nOn September 9, two fighters pulled out from their bouts: [[Brian Ortega]] and [[Bobby Green]] were expected to face [[Hacran Dias]] and [[Josh Burkman]], respectively.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/brian-ortega-out-at-ufc-fight-night-96-officials-seek-new-opponent-for-hacran-dias|title=Brian Ortega out at UFC Fight Night 96, officials seek new opponent for Hacran Dias|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-09-09|access-date=2016-09-09}}{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/bobby-green-withdraws-from-ufc-fight-night-96-ufc-needs-lightweight-to-face-josh-burkman|title=Bobby Green withdraws from UFC Fight Night 96; UFC needs lightweight to face Josh Burkman|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=John Morgan|date=2016-09-09|access-date=2016-09-09}} Ortega was replaced by [[Andre Fili]], while Green was replaced by promotional newcomer [[Zak Ottow]]. Due to the late replacement, Burkman-Ottow was contested at welterweight.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/andre-fili-steps-in-will-face-hacran-dias-at-ufc-fight-night-96|title=Andre Fili steps in, will face Hacran Dias at UFC Fight Night 96|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-09-13|access-date=2016-09-13}}{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/zak-ottow-gets-call-to-replace-bobby-green-faces-josh-burkman-at-ufc-fight-night-96|title=Zak Ottow in against Josh Burkman at UFC Fight Night 96|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Brent Brookhouse|date=2016-09-20|access-date=2016-09-20}}\n\n[[Sergio Pettis]] was expected to face [[Louis Smolka]] at the event. However, Pettis pulled out of the fight on September 22 citing what his trainer described as \"a minor injury\".{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/trainer-sergio-pettis-off-next-weeks-ufc-fight-night-96-main-card|title=Trainer: Sergio Pettis off next week's UFC Fight Night 96 main card|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2016-09-22|access-date=2016-09-22}} Smolka remained on the card and faced promotional newcomer [[Brandon Moreno]], from [[The Ultimate Fighter: Tournament of Champions]].{{cite web|url=http://www.foxsports.com/ufc/story/louis-smolka-draws-tuf-24s-brandon-moreno-as-late-notice-opponent-092316|title=Louis Smolka draws TUF 24's Brandon Moreno as late-notice opponent|work=foxsports.com|author=Damon Martin|date=2016-09-23|access-date=2016-09-23}}\n\nAt the weigh-ins, three fighters, [[John Lineker]], [[Alex Oliveira]] and [[Hacran Dias]] each missed the required weight limit for their respective fights. As a result, all three fighters were fined 20% of their fight purse, which went to their opponents.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2016/09/ufc-fight-night-96-early-weigh-in-results-and-live-video-stream-noon-et|title=UFC Fight Night 96 weigh-in results: John Lineker, Alex Oliveira, Hacran Dias all
